\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}=-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}^{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}{}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\Gamma-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\,,\qquad\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\chi-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\,,\qquad\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}=\tilde{J}^{0}\,,

(202)

where the magnetic Gauss constraint is automatically satisfied.
Otherwise, an equivalent system can be solved in terms of the electric
and magnetic fields,

\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\,,\qquad\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\,,\qquad\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}=\tilde{J}^{0}\,,\qquad\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=0\,.

(203)

This system of equations is then closed using Eq. (
188
) for the components of the
four-current
\tilde{J}^{\mu}
, as long as we can describe the evolution
of the peculiar velocity.

5.5
Displacement current and magnetic induction equation

The conductivity of the primordial plasma became
very large after the period of inflation, during the radiation-dominated epoch
of the early Universe
Arnold et al. (2000)
;
Durrer and Neronov (2013)

(see Fig.
4
).
In this limit, it is possible to neglect the displacement current
\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}
and the charge density
\tilde{\rho}_{e}

with respect to the conductivity, which is a common assumption
made in MHD
Brandenburg et al. (1996)
;
Biskamp (2003)
;
Brandenburg and Subramanian (2005)
;
Durrer and Neronov (2013)
;
Shukurov and Subramanian (2021)
.
This can be understood introducing Ohm’s law in Ampère’s law,

(\partial_{\tau}+\gamma\tilde{\sigma})\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}+\gamma\tilde{\rho}_{e}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}-\gamma\tilde{\sigma}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\,.

(204)

Comparing the two terms in brackets, we find that the displacement
current can be neglected when the time scale of the electric field
oscillations is much larger than the Faraday time

\tau_{\rm Far}=\tilde{\eta}/\gamma

Brandenburg and Subramanian (2005)
.
Under this assumption, the current density can be directly
obtained from the magnetic field using Ampère’s law,

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\,.

(205)

We note that this is valid in the large conductivity limit unless the frequency
of the electric field oscillations becomes very large.
In this limit, Ampère’s law [cf. Eq. (
205
)] becomes a constraint equation
and the
remaining dynamical equation can be obtained introducing Ohm’s law [cf. Eq. (
190
)]
in Faraday’s law [cf. Eq. (
200
)],

\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\bigl(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}-\tilde{\eta}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}/\gamma+\tilde{\eta}\,\tilde{\rho}_{e}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\bigr)\,.

(206)

This equation is commonly known as the induction equation in MHD and using
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}

gives an evolution equation for
\textstyle\tilde{B}
that only depends on
\textstyle\tilde{B}
and the peculiar velocity
\textstyle u
,

\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})+\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\gamma}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}^{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times[\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}(\tilde{\eta}/\gamma)]+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times(\tilde{\eta}\,\tilde{\rho}_{e}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{})\,.

(207)

In terms of the vector potential, the induction equation is

D_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}{})+\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\gamma}\,(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}^{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\Gamma)+\tilde{\eta}\,\tilde{\rho}_{e}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\,.

(208)

In the subrelativistic limit (
\gamma\to 1
),
for neutral plasmas (
\tilde{\rho}_{e}=0
) and homogeneous
\tilde{\eta}
,
the induction equation for
\textstyle\tilde{B}
simplifies to

\lim_{\gamma\to 1}\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})+\tilde{\eta}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}^{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\,.

(209)

Expanding the curl in Eq. (
209
), the induction equation can be expressed as

\lim_{\gamma\to 1}D_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{})+\tilde{\eta}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}^{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\,.

(210)

6
Relativistic MHD in an expanding Universe

In Sec.
3
, the equations of motion of a
fluid that does not interact with gauge fields have been presented.
For a single-fluid description of charged species, the
equations of motion become coupled to Maxwell equations, studied in an expanding background in
Sec.
5
, leading to the MHD equations.

In this section, the MHD equations are described in an expanding homogeneous and isotropic FLRW background, and the
results are extended with respect to previous work
to relativistic fluid peculiar
velocities, presenting the set of equations for
the stress-energy tensor components
\tilde{T}^{0\mu}
(conservation form)
and for the fluid primitive variables

\tilde{\rho}
and
\textstyle u
(non-conservation form), together with the electromagnetic fields
\textstyle\tilde{E}

and
\textstyle\tilde{B}
.
As in previous sections, we consider
X^{0}=\tau
,
m=1

(space-like signature), and
\alpha=1
in this section,
for compactness.

In previous numerical work using the
Pencil Code
(cf.
Brandenburg et al. (1996)
;
Brandenburg et al. (2017a)
;
Roper Pol et al. (2020b)
), the MHD equations were considered for a
radiation-dominated fluid with
c_{\rm s}^{2}={\textstyle{1\over 3}}
,
such that the system of equations becomes
conformally flat (see discussion in Sec.
3.3
).
The equations were considered
in the subrelativistic limit of fluid
perturbations in the following form,

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\ln\tilde{\rho}=-\frac{4}{3}\bigl[

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\tilde{\rho}\bigr]+\frac{1}{\tilde{\rho}}\bigl[\tilde{\eta}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}^{2}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\bigr]\,,

(211a)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}=\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}}{3}\bigl[

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\tilde{\rho}\bigr]-\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}}{\tilde{\rho}}\bigl[\tilde{\eta}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}^{2}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\bigr]-\frac{1}{4}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\ln\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{3}{4\tilde{\rho}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}+\frac{2}{\tilde{\rho}}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot(\tilde{\rho}\,\tilde{\nu}\,\boldsymbol{\tilde{\sigma}})\,,

(211b)

where
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}={\textstyle{4\over 3}}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot(2\,\tilde{\rho}\,\tilde{\nu}\,\boldsymbol{\tilde{\sigma}})
corresponds to the subrelativistic Navier-Stokes description
of the viscosity with zero bulk viscosity,
as discussed in Sec.
4
[cf. Eq. (
152b
)].

It has been already shown in Secs.
3.4
and
3.5
that this system of equations is missing
a few corrections in the purely fluid limit (i.e., in the
absence of electromagnetic fields) due to neglecting
\partial_{\tau}\gamma^{2}
, and the system of equations has been
extended to the fully relativistic regime in Eqs. (
107
) and (
113
).
Taking into account these corrections,
we find in this section the following subrelativistic
MHD equations (for
c_{\rm s}^{2}={\textstyle{1\over 3}}
) [cf. Eqs. (
248
)],
where the corrections with respect to Eqs. (
211
) are indicated in red

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\ln\tilde{\rho}=-\frac{4}{3}\bigl[

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bf{\textstyle{1\over 2}}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\tilde{\rho}\bigr]+\frac{1}{\tilde{\rho}}\bigl[\tilde{\eta}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}^{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bf-}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\bigr]\,,

(212a)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}=\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}}{3}\bigl[

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bf{\textstyle{1\over 2}}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\tilde{\rho}\bigr]-\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}}{\tilde{\rho}}\bigl[\tilde{\eta}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}^{2}+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bf{\textstyle{1\over 2}}}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\bigr]-\frac{1}{4}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\ln\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{3}{4\tilde{\rho}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}+\frac{2}{\tilde{\rho}}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot(\tilde{\rho}\,\tilde{\nu}\,\boldsymbol{\tilde{\sigma}})\,.

(212b)

The work and energy dissipation due to viscous stresses,
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}
,
and thermal conductivity,
\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{0}

[cf. Eqs. (
152
)],
have been neglected
under the assumption of small shear and bulk viscosities,
and small thermal conductivity (see Sec.
4.2
).
However, we include these contributions in the form of
\tilde{f}^{\mu}_{\rm ipf}
,
discussed in Sec.
4
, in this section for generality.
Furthermore, these equations are extended to include Hubble friction
terms for deviations of
a constant
c_{\rm s}^{2}
with respect to
{\textstyle{1\over 3}}
in this work.
In the following, we also extend the MHD equations including electromagnetic
stresses in the equations of motion in the fully relativistic regime
as in Secs.
3.4
and
3.5
.

The Maxwell equations discussed in Sec.
5

become coupled to the fluid equations
of motion via the Lorentz force
\tilde{f}^{\mu}_{\rm Lor}
as it is discussed
in Sec.
6.2
.
When the displacement current can be neglected in the limit
of large conductivity (see Sec.
5.5
), the current density is
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}
, and Maxwell equations can be combined
with Ohm’s law (see Sec.
5.3
)
into the induction equation [cf. Eq. (
207
)],

D_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{})+\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\gamma}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}^{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\bigl[\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}(\tilde{\eta}/\gamma)+\tilde{\eta}\tilde{\rho}_{e}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\bigr]\,.

(213)

On the other hand, when the displacement current cannot be neglected,
e.g., due to fast electromagnetic oscillations,
the fluid equations need to be solved together with Maxwell equations,

\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\,,\qquad\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\,,\qquad\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}=\tilde{J}^{0}\,,\qquad\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=0\,,

(214)

and the generalized Ohm’s law [cf. Eq. (
188
)], which describes the
space-time components of
\tilde{J}^{\mu}
.

6.1
Electromagnetic stress-energy tensor

The stress-energy tensor from electromagnetism can be directly computed from the Lagrangian taking
into account that Einstein equation is obtained from the Einstein-Hilbert action,

T_{\mu\nu}=g_{\mu\nu}{\cal L}-2\frac{\delta{\cal L}}{\delta g^{\mu\nu}}\,,

(215)

where the Lagrangian of electromagnetism is given in Eq. (
176
),
leading to the following stress-energy tensor,

T_{\mu\nu}^{\rm EM}=F_{\mu}^{\ \sigma}F_{\nu\sigma}-{\textstyle{1\over 4}}\,g_{\mu\nu}F^{{\lambda\sigma}}F_{{\lambda\sigma}}\,.

(216)

The electromagnetic stress-energy tensor
is traceless by construction,

T^{\rm EM}={T^{\mu}_{\ \mu}}^{\rm EM}=F^{{\mu\sigma}}F_{\mu\sigma}-{\textstyle{1\over 4}}g^{\mu}_{\ \mu}F^{\lambda\sigma}F_{\lambda\sigma}=0\,.

(217)

Therefore, the equations of motion in an expanding FLRW Universe are conformally flat
when the fluid is radiation-dominated with
c_{\rm s}^{2}={\textstyle{1\over 3}}
and the bulk
viscosity
\zeta_{\rm visc}
is zero,
making the trace of the total stress-energy tensor to vanish,
as shown in Eq. (
63
).
The comoving electromagnetic stress-energy tensor is

\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu}_{\rm EM}=a^{6}T^{\mu\nu}_{\rm EM}=\tilde{F}^{\mu\sigma}\tilde{F}^{\nu}_{\ \,\sigma}-{\textstyle{1\over 4}}\,\eta^{\mu\nu}\tilde{F}^{\lambda\sigma}F_{\lambda\sigma}\,,

(218)

where
\tilde{F}^{{\mu\nu}}=a^{4}F^{\mu\nu}
and
\tilde{F}^{\mu}_{\ \,\nu}=a^{2}F^{\mu}_{\ \,\nu}

are the comoving components of the Faraday tensor.
The contraction of the Faraday tensor with itself is

\tilde{F}^{{\lambda\sigma}}F_{\lambda\sigma}=2\tilde{F}^{0i}F_{0i}+\tilde{F}^{ij}F_{ij}=-2\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}^{2}+\varepsilon_{ijl}\varepsilon_{ijk}\tilde{B}_{k}\tilde{B}_{l}=-2\,(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}^{2}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}^{2})\,.

(219)

Note that we omit a tilde for the
F_{\mu\nu}

components since they already correspond to the comoving fields
without rescaling.
The comoving components of the electromagnetic stress-energy
tensor are

\displaystyle\tilde{T}^{00}_{\rm EM}=

\displaystyle\,\tilde{F}^{0i}\tilde{F}^{0}_{\ \,i}+{\textstyle{1\over 4}}\tilde{F}^{\lambda\sigma}F_{\lambda\sigma}={\textstyle{1\over 2}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}^{2}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}^{2})\,,

(220a)

\displaystyle\tilde{T}^{0i}_{\rm EM}=

\displaystyle-\tilde{F}^{0j}\tilde{F}^{i}_{\ \,j}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\,,

(220b)

\displaystyle\tilde{T}^{ij}_{\rm EM}=

\displaystyle\,\tilde{F}^{i0}\tilde{F}^{j}_{\ \,0}+\tilde{F}^{il}\tilde{F}^{j}_{\ \,l}-{\textstyle{1\over 4}}\tilde{F}^{\lambda\sigma}F_{\lambda\sigma}\,\delta^{ij}=-\tilde{E}^{i}\tilde{E}^{j}-\tilde{B}^{i}\tilde{B}^{j}+\tilde{T}^{00}_{\rm EM}\delta^{ij}\,,

(220c)

corresponding
\tilde{T}^{00}_{\rm EM}
to the electromagnetic energy density,

\tilde{T}^{0i}_{\rm EM}
to the Poynting vector, and

\tilde{T}^{ij}_{\rm EM}
to the electromagnetic
stresses.

The equations of motion are then equivalent to those in Eqs. (
146
) and (
148
), or Eq. (
83
) for perfect
fluids, after including the electromagnetic stress-energy tensor
components,

\partial_{\mu}\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu}=\partial_{\tau}\tilde{T}^{0\nu}+\partial_{j}\tilde{T}^{j\nu}=\tilde{f}_{H}^{\nu}+\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{\nu}\,,

(221)

where
\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu}=\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu}_{\rm pf}+\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu}_{\rm EM}
, being
\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu}_{\rm pf}=\tilde{\rho}\tilde{U}^{\mu}\tilde{U}^{\nu}+\tilde{p}\tilde{h}^{\mu\nu}
the stress-energy
tensor of a perfect fluid.
We note that, as before, the equations of motion are conformally flat with

\tilde{f}_{H}^{\mu}=0
when

\beta=4
,
\alpha=1
,

\tilde{\rho}=3\tilde{p}
, and
\tilde{\zeta}_{\rm visc}=0
.

6.2
Lorentz force in covariant formulation

The electromagnetic contribution to the conservation laws in an expanding
background can be recast in the form of a four-vector, corresponding to the
Lorentz force:

\partial_{\mu}\tilde{T}^{{\mu\nu}}_{\rm EM}=-\tilde{f}^{\nu}_{\rm Lor}=-\tilde{J}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{\nu\mu}\,,

(222)

where we have defined the comoving
Lorentz force
\tilde{f}^{\mu}_{\rm Lor}=a^{6}f^{\mu}_{\rm Lor}=\tilde{J}_{\nu}\tilde{F}^{\mu\nu}
.
To prove that the Lorentz force is equivalent to

-\partial^{\mu}\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm EM}
, the following relation
needs to be shown,

-\partial_{\mu}\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu}_{\rm EM}=-\partial_{\mu}(\tilde{F}^{\mu\sigma}\tilde{F}^{\nu}_{\ \,\sigma})-{\textstyle{1\over 4}}\,\eta^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}(\tilde{F}^{\lambda\sigma}F_{\lambda\sigma})=-\tilde{F}^{\nu}_{\ \,\sigma}\,\partial_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{\mu\sigma}=\tilde{F}^{\nu}_{\ \,\sigma}\tilde{J}^{\sigma}\,,

(223)

where we have introduced Maxwell equations
\partial_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{\mu\sigma}=-\tilde{J}^{\sigma}
.
The following relation implies the validity of Eq. (
223
),

\tilde{F}^{\mu\sigma}\partial_{\mu}F_{\nu\sigma}={\textstyle{1\over 4}}\partial_{\nu}(\tilde{F}^{\lambda\sigma}F_{\lambda\sigma})={\textstyle{1\over 2}}\tilde{F}^{\mu\sigma}\partial_{\nu}F_{\mu\sigma}\,.

(224)

To prove this relation, and hence Eq. (
223
), we first exploit the antisymmetry of the
Faraday tensor to write

\tilde{F}^{\mu\sigma}(\partial_{\mu}F_{\nu\sigma}-{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\partial_{\nu}F_{\mu\sigma})={\textstyle{1\over 2}}\tilde{F}^{\mu\sigma}(\partial_{\mu}F_{\nu\sigma}-\partial_{\mu}F_{\sigma\nu}+\partial_{\nu}F_{\sigma\mu})\,.

(225)

Then, we can apply Bianchi identities
\partial_{\mu}F_{\nu\sigma}+\partial_{\nu}F_{\sigma\mu}+\partial_{\sigma}F_{\mu\nu}=0
to
prove Eq. (
224
),

\tilde{F}^{\mu\sigma}\bigl(\partial_{\mu}F_{\nu\sigma}+{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\partial_{\nu}F_{\mu\sigma}\bigr)=-{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\tilde{F}^{\mu\sigma}\bigl(\partial_{\mu}F_{\sigma\nu}+\partial_{\sigma}F_{\mu\nu}\bigr)=0\,,

(226)

which is zero as it corresponds to the product of a symmetric and
an antisymmetric tensor.
Therefore, Eq. (
222
) is proven.
\square

The components of the Lorentz force are

\tilde{f}^{0}_{\rm Lor}=\tilde{J}_{i}\tilde{F}^{0i}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\,,\qquad\tilde{f}^{i}_{\rm Lor}=\tilde{J}_{0}\tilde{F}^{i0}+\tilde{J}_{j}\tilde{F}^{ij}=\tilde{J}^{0}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\,.

(227)

Using the covariant generalized Ohm’s law given in Eq. (
182
), the Lorentz
force can also be expressed as

\tilde{f}^{\mu}_{\rm Lor}=\tilde{J}_{\nu}\tilde{F}^{{\mu\nu}}=\tilde{\rho}_{e}\tilde{E}^{\mu}+\tilde{\sigma}\tilde{E}_{\nu}\tilde{F}^{\mu\nu}=\tilde{E}_{\nu}\,(\tilde{\rho}_{e}\eta^{\mu\nu}+\tilde{\sigma}\tilde{F}^{\mu\nu})\,.

(228)

In particular, the temporal component of the Lorentz force becomes

\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0}=\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\gamma}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}^{2}-\tilde{\eta}\,\tilde{\rho}_{e}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\,,

(229)

when expressed as a function of the current density, and

\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0}=\gamma\tilde{\rho}_{e}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}+\gamma\tilde{\sigma}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}^{2}-\gamma\tilde{\sigma}u_{i}\tilde{T}^{0i}_{\rm EM}\,,

(230)

when expressed as a function of the electric field and the Poynting vector
\tilde{T}^{0i}_{\rm EM}=(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})^{i}
.
In the subrelativistic limit, and assuming a neutral plasma (
\tilde{\rho}_{e}=0
), it reduces to

\lim_{u^{2}\ll 1}\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0}=\tilde{\eta}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle J$}}{}^{2}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})=\tilde{\sigma}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}^{2}-\tilde{\sigma}u_{i}\tilde{T}^{0i}_{\rm EM}\,.

(231)

On the other hand, the space components of the Lorentz force,
applying Ohm’s law, become

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}=\biggl(\frac{\tilde{\rho}_{e}}{\gamma}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\biggr)\biggl(\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\gamma}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}-\tilde{\eta}\tilde{\rho}_{e}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\biggr)+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\,,

(232)

expressed as a function of the current density and

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}=\gamma\tilde{\rho}_{e}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})+\gamma\tilde{\sigma}\bigl[(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{})\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}^{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\bigr]\,,

(233)

as a function of the electric field.
Again, in the subrelativistic limit, and assuming a neutral plasma, we
find

\lim_{u^{2}\ll 1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=\tilde{\sigma}\bigl[\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}^{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\bigr]\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\,,

(234)

where we have assumed that
|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}|\sim|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}|\sim{\cal O}(u)
and
omitted terms of order
{\cal O}(u^{3})
and higher.
In this limit,
\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0}=\tilde{\eta}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}^{2}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}
.
As in the case of imperfect fluids with viscous forces [cf. Eq. (
148
)], the temporal
component of the Lorentz force
\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0}
corresponds to irreversible energy
dissipation, Joule heating, and includes irreversible work done by the Lorentz force
converting electromagnetic into thermal and kinetic
energy respectively [cf. Eq. (
212a
)].
This is due to the fact that the electromagnetic stress-energy tensor cannot be
recast in the form of a perfect fluid, as can be seen from the off-diagonal non-zero
components in Eqs. (
220
).
Hence, the stress-energy tensor adds a contribution to the deviatoric
stress tensor described in Sec.
4
that leads to the production of entropy.
At the same time, this implies that net electromagnetic fields at the largest
scales of the Universe are required to vanish, to avoid breaking down the assumptions
of homogeneity and isotropy described in Sec.
2
.
Note that this does not imply that their squared fluctuations, which can be
correlated at large scales, are necessarily vanishing.

Note that we can alternatively show the relation
\partial_{\mu}\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu}_{\rm EM}=-\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{\nu}

for each component
using Maxwell equations,
\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}
and
\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}=\boldsymbol{0}
.
These relations will be useful in the following.
Let us start with the temporal component,

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\tilde{T}^{00}_{\rm EM}+\partial_{i}\tilde{T}^{0i}_{\rm EM}=

\displaystyle\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\partial_{\tau}\bigl(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}^{2}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}^{2}\bigr)+\varepsilon_{ijl}\,\partial^{i}(\tilde{E}^{j}\tilde{B}^{l})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\cdot\bigl(\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\bigr)+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\cdot\bigl(\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\bigr)=-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\,.\hskip 65.44133pt\blacksquare

(235)

This expression corresponds to Poynting’s theorem, describing the evolution
of electromagnetic energy density.
For the spatial components, let us first compute the time derivative
of the Poynting flux

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\tilde{T}^{0i}_{\rm EM}=

\displaystyle\,\partial_{\tau}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})^{i}=\varepsilon^{ijl}\,\tilde{B}_{l}\,\partial_{\tau}\tilde{E}_{j}+\varepsilon^{ijl}\,\tilde{E}_{j}\,\partial_{\tau}\tilde{B}_{l}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\tilde{B}^{j}\,\partial_{j}\tilde{B}^{i}-\tilde{B}^{j}\,\partial^{i}\tilde{B}_{j}-\tilde{E}^{j}\,\partial^{i}\tilde{E}_{j}+\tilde{E}^{j}\,\partial_{j}\tilde{E}^{i}-(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})^{i}\,.

(236)

Then,
\partial_{\mu}\tilde{T}^{\mu i}_{\rm EM}
becomes

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\tilde{T}^{0i}_{\rm EM}+\partial_{j}\tilde{T}^{ij}_{\rm EM}=

\displaystyle\,\partial_{\tau}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})^{i}-\partial_{j}(\tilde{E}^{i}\tilde{E}^{j})-\partial_{j}(\tilde{B}^{i}\tilde{B}^{j})+{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\delta^{ij}\,\partial_{i}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}^{2}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tilde{E}^{i}\,(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{})-\tilde{B}^{i}\,(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})-(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})^{i}=-\tilde{f}^{i}_{\rm Lor}\,,\hskip 85.35826pt\blacksquare

(237)

where we have used Gauss laws,
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}=\tilde{J}^{0}
and

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=0
.

6.3
Conservation form of relativistic magnetohydrodynamics

The MHD equations of motion given in Eq. (
221
) already constitute
a solvable system expressed in the conservation form.
The contribution to the stress-energy tensor from electromagnetic fields
can either be included in the dynamical variables
\tilde{T}^{0\mu}
solved in
the conservation form or be added as an external force
\tilde{f}^{\mu}_{\rm Lor}
,
as done in Sec.
3.4
for perfect fluids
and in Sec.
4
for imperfect fluids including Navier-Stokes viscosity
and Fourier’s thermal conductivity.
In the latter case, the procedure is indentical to that described in
Sec.
3.4
, and the Lorentz force is computed using
Eq. (
227
) after solving Maxwell equations.

Alternatively, if we solve for
\tilde{T}^{0\mu}=\tilde{T}^{0\mu}_{\rm pf}+\tilde{T}^{0\mu}_{\rm EM}
as the dynamic variables, we need to express

\tilde{T}^{ij}
as a function
of
\tilde{T}^{0\mu}
, as done in Sec.
3.4

for the perfect fluid system.
As we know the relation between the ratio
r^{2}
and the Lorentz factor
in terms of
\tilde{T}^{0\mu}
of the perfect fluid,
given in Eq. (
87
),
one finds

r^{2}=\frac{\tilde{T}^{0i}_{\rm pf}\,\tilde{T}^{0i}_{\rm pf}}{\bigl(\tilde{T}^{00}_{\rm pf}\bigr)^{2}}=\frac{(\tilde{T}^{0i}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\,(\tilde{T}^{0i}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})}{(\tilde{T}^{00}-\tilde{T}^{00}_{\rm EM})^{2}}=\frac{\gamma^{2}(\gamma^{2}-1)}{\Bigl(\gamma^{2}-\frac{c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\Bigr)^{2}}\,,

(238)

where the electromagnetic energy density is

\tilde{T}^{00}_{\rm EM}={\textstyle{1\over 2}}(\tilde{E}^{2}+\tilde{B}^{2})
,
and we have used an equation of state such that
\tilde{p}=c_{\rm s}^{2}\tilde{\rho}
.
Hence, once the electromagnetic fields are known,
r^{2}
can be computed,
and then one can
solve for the Lorentz factor using Eq. (
88
).
Finally,
\tilde{T}^{ij}
can be computed in the following way

\tilde{T}^{ij}=\tilde{T}^{ij}_{\rm pf}+\tilde{T}^{ij}_{\rm EM}=\bigl[(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\gamma^{2}u^{i}u^{j}+c_{\rm s}^{2}\delta^{ij}\bigr]\tilde{\rho}+\tilde{T}^{ij}_{\rm EM}\,,

(239)

with

\tilde{\rho}=\frac{\tilde{T}^{00}-\tilde{T}^{00}_{\rm EM}}{(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\gamma^{2}-c_{\rm s}^{2}}\,,\qquad u^{i}=\frac{\tilde{T}^{0i}-(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})^{i}}{(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\tilde{\rho}\gamma^{2}}\,.

(240)

This procedure allows us to compute the fully relativistic system of MHD
equations in their conservation form in an alternative way (compared to the
one in which electromagnetic contributions are included via the Lorentz
force).
Note that this procedure is generic for any contribution to
\tilde{T}^{ij}

in addition to that of a perfect fluid that does not depend on the
fluid variables.

The equations of motion of the stress-energy tensor components
are coupled to Maxwell equations
and the generalized Ohm’s law, which have been described in
Sec.
5
.
We then need to solve the MHD equations coupled to the
Maxwell equations, as given in
Sec.
5.4
,
to evolve the electric and magnetic fields,
together with the equations of motion of
\tilde{T}^{\mu 0}
or
\tilde{T}^{\mu 0}_{\rm pf}
.
One also needs to use
Ohm’s law to decribe the components of the current
density, given in Eq. (
188
), and to compute the components
of the Lorentz force, given in Eq. (
227
),
when
\tilde{T}^{\mu 0}_{\rm pf}
are evolved.

On the other hand, when the displacement current can be neglected,
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}
,
and it is enough to evolve the magnetic field using the induction
equation [cf. Eq. (
207
)], where Ohm’s law has already been applied
to describe the electric field in Maxwell equations.
The electric field can then be computed using Eq. (
190
).
In this case, the electromagnetic components of the stress-energy
tensor or the components of the Lorentz force need to be expressed
in terms of the current density applying Ohm’s law.

6.4
Non-conservation form of relativistic magnetohydrodynamics

6.4.1 Relativistic equations

The computation of the non-conservation form of the fluid equations follows an
analogous calculation to those of Secs.
3.5
and
4
, where we now
include the electromagnetic Lorentz forces, setting
\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{\mu}\to\tilde{f}_{\rm tot}^{\mu}=\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{\mu}+\tilde{f}^{\mu}_{\rm Lor}
in Eqs. (
151
).
This leads to the relativistic energy and momentum MHD equations, presented
in the introduction in Eqs. (
2
),

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\ln\tilde{\rho}\,=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-\frac{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2}}\,(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle\,+\frac{1}{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2}}\frac{1}{\tilde{\rho}}\bigl[(\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{0}+\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0})(1+u^{2})-2\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor})\bigr]+{\cal F}_{H}^{0}\,,

(241a)

\displaystyle D_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}}{(1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2})\gamma^{2}}\biggl[c_{\rm s}^{2}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+c_{\rm s}^{2}\frac{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\tilde{\rho}-\frac{1}{\tilde{\rho}}\biggl(\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{0}+\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0}-\frac{2c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot[\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}]\biggr)\biggr]

\displaystyle\,-\frac{c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\ln\tilde{\rho}}{\gamma^{2}}+\frac{1}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\,\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}}{\tilde{\rho}\gamma^{2}}+\boldsymbol{\cal F}_{H}\,,

(241b)

where the Hubble friction
{\cal F}_{H}^{\mu}

appearing in the energy and momentum equations
is given for a generic choice of
\beta
in Eqs. (
108
) and (
114
).
The imperfect (viscous) contributions to the energy and momentum equations
are described by the four-force
\tilde{f}^{\mu}_{\rm ipf}=\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu\nu}
, whose
components
for the subrelativistic Navier-Stokes viscosity and Fourier’s conductivity description are given in Eqs. (
147
) and (
148
).
The work done by the viscous forces,
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}
, is given in Eq. (
150
).
The contributions from the Lorentz force
to the energy conservation are given in Eq. (
227
).
Using Ohm’s law,
\tilde{f}^{0}_{\rm Lor}
is given by Eq. (
229
), and the
work done by the Lorentz force,
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}
, becomes

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}=\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\gamma}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{})^{2}+(1-2u^{2})\,\tilde{\eta}\,\tilde{\rho}_{e}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}-\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\gamma}\,\tilde{\rho}_{e}^{2}\,u^{2}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\,.

(242)

The energy conservation equation contains irreversible Joule heating

\tilde{\eta}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}^{2}\supset\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0}
, already introduced
in previous work with
Pencil Code
, while it also contains the work
exerted by the Lorentz force
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\supset\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}
, which converts electromagnetic energy into kinetic energy
when
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}>0
and kinetic into electromagnetic
when
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}<0
.
In this work, we also include the viscous contributions, as well as the
general
\tilde{f}^{0}_{\rm Lor}
and
