\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}
for relativistic
bulk motion and
\tilde{\rho}_{e}\neq 0
.

6.4.2 Evolution of the Lorentz factor

Similarly as for perfect and imperfect fluids, the time derivative of the
squared Lorentz factor contains subrelativistic terms.
The generic time derivative of the Lorentz factor, in the full relativistic
regime, is obtained by adding
\tilde{f}^{\mu}_{\rm Lor}

to the Hubble and viscous forces in
Eqs. (
119
) and (
149
), respectively

D_{\tau}\ln\gamma^{2}\to[D_{\tau}\ln\gamma^{2}]_{\rm ipf}-\frac{2\,u^{2}}{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2}}\frac{\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0}}{\tilde{\rho}}+\frac{2}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\,\frac{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2}}{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2}}\,\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}}{\tilde{\rho}}\,.

(243)

In the subrelativistic limit, the contributions to
\partial_{\tau}\ln\gamma^{2}
due to electromagnetic forces (and any other potential
forces in the system) are then non-negligible,

\lim_{u^{2}\ll 1}D_{\tau}\ln\gamma^{2}=-\frac{2c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\bigl(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\bigr)\ln\tilde{\rho}+\frac{2}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor})}{\tilde{\rho}}\,,

(244)

leading to a correction in the
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})
terms
in Eqs. (
211
), as shown in red in Eqs. (
212
),
that was overlooked in previous work.

6.4.3 Subrelativistic limit

The subrelativistic limit of Eqs. (
241
) is

\displaystyle\lim_{u^{2}\ll 1}\partial_{\tau}\ln\tilde{\rho}\,=

\displaystyle\,-(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-(1-c_{\rm s}^{2})(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle\,+\frac{1}{\tilde{\rho}}\bigl[\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{0}+\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0}-2\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor})\bigr]+\bigl[\beta-3\,(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\bigr]{\cal H}\,,

(245a)

\displaystyle\lim_{u^{2}\ll 1}D_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}=

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\,c_{\rm s}^{2}\biggl[\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+\frac{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\tilde{\rho}\biggr]-\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}}{\tilde{\rho}}\biggl[\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{0}+\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0}-\frac{2\,c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor})\biggr]

\displaystyle\,-\frac{c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\ln\tilde{\rho}+\frac{1}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}}{\tilde{\rho}}+(3c_{\rm s}^{2}-1)\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\,{\cal H}\,.

(245b)

The Lorentz-force components
\tilde{f}^{0}_{\rm Lor}
[cf. Eq. (
229
)]
and
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}
[cf. Eq. (
242
)]
can be expressed up to leading-order in
u^{2}
as

\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0}=\tilde{\eta}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}^{2}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})-\tilde{\eta}\,\tilde{\rho}_{e}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\,,\qquad\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})+\tilde{\eta}\,\tilde{\rho}_{e}\,(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}-\tilde{\rho}_{e}u^{2})+{\cal O}(u^{3})\,,

(246)

where we keep terms up to order
{\cal O}(u^{2})
under
the assumption
|J|\sim{\cal O}(u)
.
Then, the terms appearing in the subrelativistic energy and momentum equations are

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0}-2\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}=

\displaystyle\,\tilde{\eta}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}^{2}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})-3\tilde{\eta}\tilde{\rho}_{e}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}+2\tilde{\eta}\tilde{\rho}_{e}^{2}u^{2}+{\cal O}(u^{3})\,,

(247a)

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0}-\frac{2\,c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}=

\displaystyle\,\tilde{\eta}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}^{2}+\frac{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})

\displaystyle\,-\frac{\tilde{\eta}\tilde{\rho}_{e}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\bigl[(1+3c_{\rm s}^{2})\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}+2c_{\rm s}^{2}\tilde{\rho}_{e}u^{2}\bigr]+{\cal O}(u^{3})\,.

(247b)

In particular, when the plasma is quasi-neutral (
\tilde{\rho}_{e}\simeq 0
), the subrelativistic MHD equations in an expanding background
become

\displaystyle\lim_{u^{2}\ll 1}\partial_{\tau}\ln\tilde{\rho}\,=

\displaystyle\,-(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}(1-c_{\rm s}^{2})}\,(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\tilde{\rho}+\frac{1}{\tilde{\rho}}(\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{0}-2\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf})

\displaystyle\,+\frac{1}{\tilde{\rho}}\bigl[\tilde{\eta}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}^{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\bigr]+\bigl[\beta-3(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\bigr]\,{\cal H}\,,

(248a)

\displaystyle\lim_{u^{2}\ll 1}D_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}=

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\,c_{\rm s}^{2}\biggl[\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\frac{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\tilde{\rho}\biggr]-\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}}{\tilde{\rho}}\biggl[\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{0}-\frac{2\,c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}+\tilde{\eta}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}^{2}+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\frac{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\bigl(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\bigr)\biggr]

\displaystyle\,-\frac{c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\ln\tilde{\rho}+\frac{1}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}}{\tilde{\rho}}+(3c_{\rm s}^{2}-1)\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\,{\cal H}\,,

(248b)

where the correction with respect to previous work is indicated in red.
These equations

reduce to Eqs. (
212
) for a viscous
fluid with
c_{\rm s}^{2}={\textstyle{1\over 3}}
, omitting viscous effects in the
energy conservation equation.

6.5
Transverse Alfvén waves

In this section, we study one of the most relevant solutions
in an MHD system, transverse
Alfvén waves, with the objective to review the
dependence of the Alfvén speed on the
value of
c_{\rm s}^{2}
for a constant equation of state
\tilde{p}=c_{\rm s}^{2}\tilde{\rho}

Jedamzik et al. (1998)
;
Subramanian and Barrow (1998)
and show how the Alfvén speed can become
superluminal when one neglects the displacement current, even if
the bulk velocity is subrelativistic.
This issue can be overcome using the Boris correction
Boris (1970)
, which
will be discussed in Sec.
6.6
and extended to the
relativistic MHD equations in the early Universe.

For simplicity, we will ignore dissipative effects like viscosity and
magnetic diffusivity and consider a neutral plasma with
\tilde{\rho}_{e}=0
.
We also neglect the Hubble friction, which vanishes for a radiation-dominated fluid and, otherwise, complicates the computation of the
perturbations due to the time-dependence of the Hubble rate
{\cal H}
.
However, the procedure to compute the perturbations including the expansion
of the Universe follows the one presented in Sec.
3.7
.
For this reason, in this and Sec.
6.7
,
we drop the tildes in the comoving variables,
as the Universe expansion is neglected.
Then, the subrelativistic ideal
MHD equations are
[cf. Eqs. (
248
)]

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\ln\rho\,+

\displaystyle\,(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+(1-c_{\rm s}^{2})\,\bigl(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\bigr)\ln\rho=-\frac{1}{\rho}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\bigl(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle J$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}\bigr)\,,

(249a)

\displaystyle D_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}=

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\,c_{\rm s}^{2}\biggl[\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+\frac{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\rho\biggr]-\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}}{\rho}\,\frac{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\bigl(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle J$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}\bigr)

\displaystyle\,-\frac{c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\ln\rho+\frac{1}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle J$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}}{\rho}\,.

(249b)

When the displacement current can be neglected, in the limit
\eta\to 0
,
the magnetic field evolution is governed by the ideal induction equation [cf. Eq. (
213
)],

D_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}=(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{})\,.

(250)

Let us consider linear perturbations above a background
homogeneous magnetic field
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}_{0}=B_{0}\,\hat{e}_{z}
,

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}_{0}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}_{1}e^{i\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}{}-i\omega\tau}
,
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}_{1}e^{i\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}{}-i\omega\tau}
,
\rho=\rho_{0}+\rho_{1}e^{i\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}{}-i\omega\tau}
, where the
perturbations occur in the direction of the homogeneous magnetic
field and in the plane
perpendicular to it,
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}{}=k_{\parallel}\hat{e}_{z}+k_{\perp}\hat{e}_{y}
.
The induction equation yields,

\omega\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}_{1}=-(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}_{0}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}{})\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}_{1}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}_{0}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}_{1})=B_{0}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-k_{\parallel}u_{x}\\
-k_{\parallel}u_{y}\\
\ \,k_{\perp}u_{y}\end{array}\right)\,.

(251)

The current density is in this case

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle J$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}=i\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}k_{\perp}B_{z}-k_{\parallel}B_{y}\\
\ k_{\parallel}B_{x}\\
-k_{\perp}B_{x}\end{array}\right)\,,

(252)

such that the Lorentz force components are (up to first-order),
f_{\rm Lor}^{0}=0
, and

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle f$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle f$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle f$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle f$}}{}_{\rm Lor}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle J$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle J$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}=iB_{0}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}k_{\parallel}B_{x}\\
k_{\parallel}B_{y}-k_{\perp}B_{z}\\
0\end{array}\right)\,.

(253)

Note that
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle f$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle f$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle f$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle f$}}{}_{\rm Lor}

only contains terms of second and higher order, so it can be neglected.

Transverse Alfvén waves correspond to fluid perturbations
in the direction perpendicular to both
\textstyle k
and
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}_{0}
, i.e.,
u_{x}
.
The momentum equation in this direction, neglecting all terms of
second or higher order, is

\omega u_{x}+k_{\parallel}\frac{B_{x}B_{0}}{(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\rho_{0}}=0\Rightarrow u_{x}\bigl(\omega^{2}-k_{\parallel}^{2}v_{\rm A}^{2}\bigr)=0\,,

(254)

which corresponds to transverse Alfvén waves with group
speed

v_{\rm A}^{2}=\frac{B_{0}^{2}}{(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\rho_{0}}\,.

(255)

Note that in regions where the energy density is very small or the magnetic field is very large, the
Alfvén speed can grow unbounded, potentially leading to superluminal Alfvén speeds.
This is a consequence of neglecting the displacement current,
as indicated in
Boris (1970)
, and the Alfvén speed
can be corrected including the Boris correction in the momentum
equation, as we will show in the next section.
Magnetosonic waves, i.e., perturbations in the plane formed by
\textstyle k
and
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}_{0}
,
are considered in Sec.
6.7
.

6.6
Boris correction for relativistic Alfvén speeds

In MHD simulations, it is common to neglect the displacement current,
as the conductivity is usually larger than the oscillation frequency
of the electric field (see Sec.
5.5
).
This is particularly true in the early Universe, where
\eta
is usually
several orders of magnitude smaller than the Hubble size (see Fig.
4
).
However, we have shown that the resulting
Alfvén waves can propagate
faster than the speed of light, independently of the values
of the bulk velocity [cf. Eq. (
255
)].
To correct this effect in the limit of subrelativistic bulk motion,

u^{2}\ll 1
, the Boris correction was proposed in
Boris (1970)
.
In the following, we reproduce the
Boris correction adapted to the MHD
system in an expanding Universe with a relativistic equation of state,
such that
\tilde{p}=c_{\rm s}^{2}\tilde{\rho}
with constant
c_{\rm s}^{2}
.
Note that in this section, all the assumptions taken to study
Alfvén waves in the previous section are dropped, and only recovered
at the end of the section when the application of Boris correction
to Alfvén waves is taken into account.

In first place, note that the Lorentz force that appears in the
momentum equation can be recast in terms of the divergence of the
electromagnetic tensor,
\tilde{f}^{i}_{\rm Lor}=-\partial_{\mu}\tilde{T}^{i\mu}_{\rm EM}
[cf. Eq. (
237
)],

\tilde{f}^{i}_{\rm Lor}=-\partial_{\tau}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})_{i}-\partial_{j}\tilde{T}^{ij}_{\rm EM}\,.

(256)

In the limit of ideal MHD (
\tilde{\eta}\to 0
), we can set
\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle E$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle E$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle E$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}}{}=-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}
from Ohm’s law,
such that quadratic terms in
\tilde{E}_{i}
are
proportional to
u^{2}
and, hence, of a higher order than
the time derivative
of the Poynting vector and the magnetic stresses.

Then, the
subrelativistic momentum equation in this limit can be expressed as
[cf. Eq. (
245b
)],

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\biggl[\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-\frac{(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}}{(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\tilde{\rho}}\biggr]=

\displaystyle-(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\,c_{\rm s}^{2}\biggl[\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+\frac{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\tilde{\rho}\biggr]

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}}{\tilde{\rho}}\biggl[\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{0}-\frac{2\,c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}+\tilde{\eta}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}^{2}+\frac{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\biggr]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\tilde{\rho}}\Bigl(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tilde{P}}+\bigl[(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\bigr]\partial_{\tau}\ln\tilde{\rho}\Bigr)+(3c_{\rm s}^{2}-1)\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\,{\cal H}\,,

(257)

where the total comoving
pressure tensor
\boldsymbol{\tilde{P}}
incorporates the isotropic fluid pressure, the
deviatoric viscous stresses, and the magnetic field pressure,

\tilde{P}_{ij}=\bigl(\tilde{p}-\tilde{\zeta}_{\rm visc}\,\theta+{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}^{2}\bigr)\,\delta_{ij}-2\,\tilde{\eta}_{\rm visc}\,\tilde{\sigma}_{ij}-\tilde{B}_{i}\tilde{B}_{j}\,,

(258)

omitting the electric field pressure
-\tilde{E}_{i}\tilde{E}_{j}+{1\over 2}\delta_{ij}\tilde{E}^{2}\sim{\cal O}(u^{2})
.
The
\partial_{\tau}\ln\tilde{\rho}
term in Eq. (
257
) is obtained from the
subrelativistic energy equation [cf. Eq. (
248a
)].

The term in the left-hand-side of Eq. (
257
) can be expressed as
Boris (1970)
;
Gombosi et al. (2002)
;
Chatterjee (2020)

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-\frac{(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}}{(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\tilde{\rho}}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}(1+v_{\rm A}^{2})-v_{\rm A}^{2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{b}}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{})\,\hat{\boldsymbol{b}}=u_{j}\bigl[\delta_{ij}+v_{\rm A}^{2}(\delta_{ij}-\hat{b}_{i}\hat{b}_{j})\bigr]\,,

(259)

where we have defined the Alfvén speed
v_{\rm A}^{2}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}^{2}/[(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\tilde{\rho}]

and
\hat{\boldsymbol{b}}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}/|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}|
is the unitary magnetic field.
In matrix form, this can be expressed as

\partial_{\tau}\biggl[\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-\frac{(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}}{(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\tilde{\rho}}\biggr]=\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\,\bigl[{\boldsymbol{I}}+v_{\rm A}^{2}({\boldsymbol{I}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{b}}\hat{\boldsymbol{b}})\bigr]\,,

(260)

where we have neglected the following term

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\,\partial_{\tau}\bigl[v_{\rm A}^{2}({\boldsymbol{I}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{b}}\hat{\boldsymbol{b}})\bigr]\sim u_{j}B^{i}\partial_{\tau}B^{j}\sim u_{j}B^{i}\,(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times E)^{j}\sim{\cal O}(u^{2})\,,

(261)

which is of second order in
u^{2}
in the ideal MHD limit.
The matrix in Eq. (
260
)
has the following inverse

\bigl[{\boldsymbol{I}}+v_{\rm A}^{2}({\boldsymbol{I}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{b}}\hat{\boldsymbol{b}})\bigr]^{-1}={\boldsymbol{I}}-v_{\rm A}^{2}\gamma_{A}({\boldsymbol{I}}-\hat{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle b$}}}{}\hat{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle b$}}}{})\,,

(262)

where

\gamma_{A}=\frac{1}{1+v_{\rm A}^{2}}\,,

(263)

is the relativistic Alfvén correction.
Then, the momentum equation can be expressed in the following way,

\partial_{\tau}u^{i}={\rm RHS}^{i}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}})-v_{\rm A}^{2}\gamma_{A}(\delta^{ij}-\hat{b}^{i}\hat{b}^{j})\,{\rm RHS}_{j}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}})={\rm RHS}^{i}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{})+F_{\rm corr}^{i}\,,

(264)

where RHS
(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{})
is the right-hand side of Eq. (
257
)
and
{\boldsymbol{F}}_{\rm corr}
is the Boris correction for relativistic Alfvén speeds.

Let us now focus on Alfvén transverse waves, studied in Sec.
6.5
, under this correction.
When we include the subrelativistic term of the displacement current,
i.e., the time derivative of the Poynting vector, in the momentum
equation, the additional term in Eq. (
257
) leads to the modification of the left-hand-side
\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\to\gamma_{A}^{-1}\,\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}
(cf. Eq. (
259
) applied to direction perpendicular
to the homogeneous magnetic field).
Then, the dispersion relation in Eq. (
254
) becomes

u_{x}\bigl(\omega^{2}-k_{\parallel}^{2}v_{\rm A}^{2}\gamma_{A}\bigr)=0\,,

(265)

and the transverse Alfvén speed gets corrected:
v_{\rm A}^{2}\to v_{\rm A}^{2}\gamma_{\rm A}=v_{\rm A}^{2}/(1+v_{\rm A}^{2})\leq 1

Boris (1970)
, ensuring subluminal
propagation of the MHD perturbations.

6.7
Magnetosonic waves

In this section, we focus on the perturbations introduced in Sec.
6.5
when the velocity field is
in the plane perpendicular to the transverse Alfvén waves, i.e., the plane defined by the direction of the perturbations
and the direction of the homogeneous background magnetic field.
As done for sound waves in Sec.
3.7
,
we define the energy density fluctuations,

\lambda=\rho_{1}/[(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\rho_{0}]
.
In the linearized theory, the energy equation does not contain contributions
from the electromagnetic fields, as they are of higher-order and one
finds [cf. Eq. (
130
)],

\displaystyle\lambda=\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}}{\omega}\,.

(266)

The momentum equation in the
y
-direction, parallel to the
perturbations,
\hat{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}{}_{\perp}
, is

\displaystyle-i\omega u_{y}\gamma_{A}^{-1}=

\displaystyle-i\,k_{\perp}\,c_{\rm s}^{2}\lambda+i\,\frac{k_{\parallel}B_{y}-k_{\perp}B_{z}}{(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\rho_{0}}B_{0}

\displaystyle\,\Rightarrow u_{y}\bigl(\omega^{2}-k^{2}c_{\rm ms}^{2}\gamma_{A}+k_{\parallel}^{2}c_{\rm s}^{2}\gamma_{A}\bigr)-u_{z}k_{\perp}k_{\parallel}c_{\rm s}^{2}\gamma_{A}=0\,,

(267)

where we have defined the magnetosonic speed
c_{\rm ms}^{2}=c_{\rm s}^{2}+v_{\rm A}^{2}
,
and already introduced the Boris correction.
The momentum equation in the parallel direction to
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle B$}}{}_{0}
is

\displaystyle-i\omega u_{z}\gamma_{A}^{-1}=-i\,k_{\parallel}c_{\rm s}^{2}\lambda\,\Rightarrow u_{z}\bigl(\omega^{2}-k_{\parallel}^{2}c_{\rm s}^{2}\gamma_{A}\bigr)-u_{y}k_{\perp}k_{\parallel}c_{\rm s}^{2}\gamma_{A}=0\,.

(268)

Hence, in matrix form, the perturbations can be expressed as

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\omega^{2}-k^{2}c_{\rm ms}^{2}\gamma_{A}+k_{\parallel}^{2}c_{\rm s}^{2}\gamma_{A}&-k_{\perp}k_{\parallel}c_{\rm s}^{2}\gamma_{A}\\
-k_{\perp}k_{\parallel}c_{\rm s}^{2}\gamma_{A}&\omega^{2}-k_{\parallel}^{2}c_{\rm s}^{2}\gamma_{A}\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{y}\\
u_{z}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
0\end{array}\right)\,.

(269)

The eigenvalues of the equation yielding the dispersion relation are obtained
when the determinant vanishes

\omega^{4}-\omega^{2}\gamma_{A}k^{2}c_{\rm ms}^{2}+k^{2}k_{\parallel}^{2}v_{\rm A}^{2}c_{\rm s}^{2}\gamma_{A}^{2}=0\,.

(270)

This expression
corresponds to magnetosonic waves.
The solution to the dispersion relation is

\omega^{2}_{\pm}={\textstyle{1\over 2}}\gamma_{A}k^{2}c_{\rm ms}^{2}\pm\gamma_{A}k\sqrt{{\textstyle{1\over 4}}k^{2}\,c_{\rm ms}^{4}-k_{\parallel}^{2}v_{\rm A}^{2}c_{\rm s}^{2}}\,.

(271)

The two solution branches of the dispersion relation correspond to the fast (
+
)
and slow (
-
) magnetosonic waves in the plasma, for the positive and negative
sign, respectively.

In particular, the perturbations propagating at an angle
\theta

with respect to the homogeneous magnetic field
can be described setting
k_{\parallel}^{2}/k^{2}=\cos^{2}\theta
,

\omega^{2}_{\pm}={\textstyle{1\over 2}}\gamma_{A}k^{2}c_{\rm ms}^{2}\pm\gamma_{A}k^{2}\sqrt{{\textstyle{1\over 4}}c_{\rm ms}^{4}-\cos^{2}\theta v_{\rm A}^{2}c_{\rm s}^{2}}\,.

(272)

In the perpendicular direction,
\theta=\pi/2
,
the slow magnetosonic wave does not propagate (
\omega_{-}=0
)
and the angular frequency of the fast magnetosonic wave is

\omega^{2}_{+}=\gamma_{A}k^{2}c_{\rm ms}^{2}\,.

(273)

7
Conclusions and summary

In the present work, the relativistic magnetohydrodynamic (MHD) equations for perfect and
imperfect (viscous) plasmas
in the early Universe are
studied in an expanding background described by the Friedmann-Lemaître-Robertson-Walker (FLRW) metric tensor under the assumptions of homogeneity and isotropy of the early
Universe.
The MHD equations in an expanding background presented in this work
are relevant to study the
dynamics of the primordial plasma during the epoch when the Universe
was dominated by radiation, allowing to include a matter component by considering small
deviations with respect to
c_{\rm s}^{2}={\textstyle{1\over 3}}
, where the square of the speed of sound
is assumed to be a constant relating the pressure and total energy
density
p=c_{\rm s}^{2}\rho
(see Sec.
3.1
for details on the
equation of state).

After a brief review of the FLRW geometry in Sec.
2
,
the relativistic equations for perfect fluids are presented
both for the purely fluid dynamical
system (i.e., in the absence of electromagnetic fields or for non-conducting fluids)
and for the MHD system.
In terms of the conformal time
\tau
, the equations of motion of the fluid in an expanding
Universe
become equivalent to those in flat Minkowski space-time after a conformal mapping of the stress-energy tensor,

\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu}=a^{6}T^{\mu\nu}
, as long as the stress-energy tensor is traceless,
which is accomplished only when
p={\textstyle{1\over 3}}\rho
,
i.e.,
c_{\rm s}^{2}={\textstyle{1\over 3}}

Brandenburg et al. (1996)
;
Subramanian and Barrow (1998)

and when the bulk viscosity

\zeta_{\rm visc}
is zero (see Stokes assumption in Sec.
4
).
The conformal transformation
\tilde{T}^{{\mu\nu}}=a^{6}T^{{\mu\nu}}
leads to the definition of the comoving energy density and pressure,
\tilde{\rho}=a^{4}\rho
and

\tilde{p}=a^{4}p
, and the comoving shear viscosity
\tilde{\nu}=a^{-1}\nu
.
In Sec.
3.3
, we explore different scalings of the fluid variables that lead to equations
with a generic Hubble friction term.
In particular, when the bulk velocities are subrelativistic,
u^{2}\ll 1
, it is found
that the scaling
\tilde{p}=a^{\beta}p
and
\tilde{\rho}=a^{\beta}\rho
with
\beta=3(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})
is the most
convenient choice as it maximizes the number of
Hubble friction terms that vanish in the fluid
equations.
A special case corresponds to a fluid composed by massive particles (dust) with

c_{\rm s}^{2}\ll 1
, for which it is convenient to separately scale the pressure and the energy
density, as they become decoupled in the fluid equations.
We show that
the equations become conformally flat choosing super-comoving
coordinates
Martel and Shapiro (1998)
, corresponding to
scaling
\tilde{p}=a^{5}p
and
\tilde{u}^{i}=au^{i}
, together with
a scaled
\alpha
-time
\tau_{\alpha}
with

\alpha=2
, defined such that
{\rm\,d}\tau_{\alpha}=a^{\alpha-1}{\rm\,d}\tau
.
An alternative choice of super-comoving coordinates corresponds to scaling

\tilde{\rho}=a^{3}\rho

and
\tilde{p}=a^{4}p

Banerjee and Jedamzik (2004)
,
together with a scaled
\alpha
-time,
{\rm\,d}\tau_{\alpha}=a^{\alpha-1}{\rm\,d}\tau
with
\alpha={\textstyle{3\over 2}}
.
This choice allows us to keep a constant value
of
{\cal H}_{\alpha}=(\partial_{\tau_{\alpha}}a)/a
and
minimizes the appearances of Hubble friction terms in the
equations if one further scales the velocity field
as
\tilde{u}^{i}=a^{\delta}u^{i}
with
\delta={\textstyle{1\over 2}}
.
We present a generalized version of the latter choice of super-comoving coordinates for
other values of the background equation of state
w\neq 0

determining the evolution of the Universe (see Sec.
2
).
These choices are explained in Sec.
3.3
and summarized in Table
1
.
The choice
\beta=3\,(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})
is especially useful when studying sound
waves in an expanding Universe, considered in Sec.
3.7
.

In Sec.
3.4
, we compute the equations of motion considering
the
\tilde{T}^{0\mu}
components of the perfect
fluid as the dynamical variables (conservation form).
We extend the conservation form of the equations of
motion to include out-of-equilibrium effects in Sec.
4
.
We present the deviatoric stress-energy tensor arising in first-order
fluid dynamics
in Sec.
4.1
, which corresponds to Navier-Stokes viscosity and Fourier’s heat
conductivity, and review in Sec.
4.2

the estimates for the transport coefficients describing out-of-equilibrium
effects in the fluid: kinematic shear viscosity (
\nu
),
shown in Fig.
3
, kinematic bulk viscosity (
\xi
), and
thermal conductivity (
\kappa
).
We include
the Lorentz force due to coupling of the fluid
with gauge fields in
Sec.
6.3
.
We summarize these equations in Sec.
7.1
.
Then, in Sec.
3.5
, we present the non-conservation form of the
equations of motion of a perfect fluid
in terms of the primitive fluid
variables: the comoving
energy density
\tilde{\rho}
and the peculiar velocity
u^{i}
.
We again include imperfect (viscous) forces in Sec.
4
and
extend to the non-conservation form of the MHD equations in Sec.
6.4
.
We summarize the non-conservation form of the MHD
equations in Sec.
7.2
.
For both approaches, we correctly incorporate the leading-order corrections
in the subrelativistic limit, which had been overlooked in previous work
as a consequence of setting
D_{\tau}\gamma^{2}\to 0
.
We explicitly show that
D_{\tau}\gamma^{2}
contains terms that are of leading order
in the subrelativistic limit, and present the corresponding corrections to the
equations of conservation of energy and momentum.

When we include the Lorentz force (see Sec.
6.2
) in the fluid
equations, the equations of motion become coupled to
Maxwell equations via the current density, which is described
using the generalized Ohm’s law, reviewed in Sec.
5.3
.
Since the trace of the electromagnetic stress-energy tensor
is zero (see Sec.
6.1
), the MHD equations are still conformally flat when

c_{\rm s}^{2}={\textstyle{1\over 3}}
and
\zeta_{\rm visc}=0
, and the
transformation
\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu}=a^{6}T^{\mu\nu}
leads to the
definition of comoving electric and magnetic fields,

\tilde{B}^{i}=a^{2}\bar{B}^{i}={\textstyle{1\over 2}}\varepsilon^{ijk}F_{jk}
and
\tilde{E}_{i}=a^{2}\bar{E}_{i}=F_{i0}

(see Secs.
5.1
and
5.2
).
We review in Sec.
5.4

Maxwell equations in an expanding Universe.
The conformal invariance of Maxwell equations implies a transformation

\tilde{J}^{\mu}=a^{4}J^{\mu}
of the four-current,
which leads to the definition of a comoving charge density

\tilde{\rho}_{e}=a^{3}\rho_{e}
and a comoving conductivity

\tilde{\sigma}=a\sigma
.
In Sec.
5.5
, we discuss the large-conductivity limit,
commonly assumed in MHD,
in which we can neglect the displacement current and
reduce Maxwell equations to the magnetic induction equation.
We present an estimate of the conductivity in the early Universe (see Fig.
4
,
which shows the magnetic diffusivity
\eta=1/\sigma
), showing
that this limit is, in general, valid during the radiation-dominated era.
We summarize Maxwell equations and the induction
equation in Sec.
7.3
.

We have reviewed the system of linearized waves in MHD.
In Sec.
3.7
, we describe sound waves in an expanding Universe,
and we describe transverse Alfvén waves and
magnetosonic waves in Secs.
6.5
and
6.7
when
the equations of motion are conformally flat.
The resulting Alfvén speed
v_{\rm A}
is shown to potentially
become superluminal when
B_{0}/\rho_{0}\gtrsim 1
even
in the regime of linearized fluid perturbations.
In Sec.
6.6
,
we provide a correction to the equation of momentum
conservation in the fluid that allows to find the correct
relativistic Alfvén speed,
v_{\rm A}\to v_{\rm A}\gamma_{A}
with

\gamma_{A}=(1+v_{\rm A}^{2})^{-1}
, following
the original Boris correction
Boris (1970)
.

7.1
Relativistic MHD equations in the conservation form

In the conservation form, the comoving
\tilde{T}^{0\mu}
components of the
stress-energy tensor
can be dynamically evolved in conformal time
using the following conservation laws (see Sec.
6

for details),

\partial_{\tau}\tilde{T}^{0\mu}_{\rm pf}+\partial_{j}\tilde{T}^{j\mu}_{\rm pf}=\tilde{f}_{H}^{\mu}+\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{\mu}+\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{\mu}\,,

(274)

where
\tilde{T}^{{\mu\nu}}_{\rm pf}=a^{4}T^{\mu\nu}_{\rm pf}
is the perfect
fluid stress-energy tensor (see Sec.
3.2
),

\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu}_{\rm pf}=(\tilde{p}+\tilde{\rho})\tilde{U}^{\mu}\tilde{U}^{\nu}+\tilde{p}\,\eta^{\mu\nu}\,,

(275)

with
\tilde{U}^{\mu}=\gamma(1,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{})
being the comoving four-velocity (see Sec.
2.4
).

The imperfect (viscous) forces have been considered following first-order fluid dynamics,
in which small deviations with respect to the perfect fluid local thermal equilibrium (LTE) are
included and modeled using the deviatoric stress tensor
\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu\nu}
, such that
the imperfect fluid stress-energy tensor is

\tilde{T}_{\rm Fl}^{\mu\nu}\equiv\tilde{T}_{\rm pf}^{\mu\nu}-\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu\nu}
, and incorporates Navier-Stokes viscosity and Fourier’s heat conductivity (see Sec.
4
).
Then, the imperfect four-force in Eq. (
274
)
is
\tilde{f}^{\mu}_{\rm ipf}=\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu\nu}
.
In the subrelativistic limit, and for negligible
bulk viscosity,
\xi H\ll 1
,
the temporal component of the imperfect four-force corresponds to the divergence
of the heat flux and the spatial components to the Navier-Stokes viscous force (i.e., the divergence
of the shear stresses) [cf. Eqs. (
147
) and (
148
)]

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{0}=\tilde{\kappa}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}^{2}\tilde{T}\,,\qquad\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{{\rm ipf}}}{\tilde{p}+\tilde{\rho}}=

\displaystyle\,\tilde{\nu}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}^{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+\bigl({\textstyle{1\over 3}}\tilde{\nu}+\tilde{\xi}\bigr)\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\tilde{\theta}+\bigl[(2\,\tilde{\nu}\,\boldsymbol{\tilde{\sigma}}+\tilde{\xi}\,\tilde{\theta}\,\boldsymbol{I})\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\bigr]\ln\tilde{\rho}\,,

(276)

where
\tilde{S}^{ij}={\textstyle{1\over 2}}(\partial^{i}u^{j}+\partial^{j}u^{i}+2\,{\cal H}\,\delta^{ij})

is the comoving
rate-of-strain tensor,
\tilde{\sigma}^{ij}=\tilde{S}^{ij}

-{\textstyle{1\over 3}}\,\tilde{\theta}\,\delta^{ij}
its traceless counterpart,
\tilde{\theta}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+3{\cal H}
the fluid
expansion scalar, and homogeneous
\tilde{\nu}
,
\tilde{\xi}
, and
\tilde{\kappa}
are assumed.
The comoving temperature
\tilde{T}=aT
can be related to the (radiation) energy density via Eq. (
156
) to compute
\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{0}

when the thermal conductivity
\kappa
is not vanishing.

The Hubble friction
\tilde{f}_{H}^{\mu}
in Eq. (
274
)
appears due to the expansion of the Universe [cf. Eq. (
84
)]

\tilde{f}_{H}^{0}=\bigl[(\beta-4)\,\tilde{T}^{00}_{\rm Fl}-\tilde{T}_{\rm Fl}\bigr]{\cal H}\,,\qquad\tilde{f}_{H}^{i}=\,(\beta-4)\,\tilde{T}^{0i}_{\rm Fl}\,{\cal H}\,,

(277)

when the comoving energy density and pressure are rescaled as
\tilde{p}=a^{\beta}p

and
\tilde{\rho}=a^{\beta}\rho
(see Sec.
3.3
).
The choice
\beta=4

is an appropriate choice for relativistic fluids, especially when
the trace of the fluid stress-energy tensor
\tilde{T}_{\rm Fl}=3\,\tilde{p}-\tilde{\rho}-3\,\tilde{\zeta}_{\rm visc}\,\tilde{\theta}
vanishes, since then
\tilde{f}_{H}^{\mu}=0
and the equations
of motion become conformally flat [cf. Eq. (
63
)].
This occurs for
an equation of state described by a constant
c_{\rm s}^{2}={\textstyle{1\over 3}}

(see Sec.
3.3
) and when the bulk viscosity is zero.
The conformal Hubble rate is
{\cal H}=a^{\prime}/a
.

Finally, the electromagnetic
Lorentz four-force is described in Sec.
6.2
,

\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\,,\qquad\tilde{f}^{i}_{\rm Lor}=\tilde{J}^{0}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\,.

(278)

The four-current
\tilde{J}^{\mu}
couples the equations of motion of the fluid with
the electric and magnetic fields,
and can be computed using
the generalized Ohm’s law
(see Sec.
5.3
),

\tilde{J}^{0}=\gamma(\tilde{\rho}_{e}+\tilde{\sigma}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{})\,,\qquad\tilde{J}^{i}=\gamma(\tilde{\rho}_{e}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+\tilde{\sigma}[\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}])\,.

(279)

The electromagnetic fields are then evolved together
with the equations of motion of the fluid using Maxwell
equations.

One last step is still required to evolve Eq. (
274
),
since
\tilde{T}^{ij}_{\rm pf}
needs to be expressed in terms of the
dynamical components
\tilde{T}^{0\mu}_{\rm pf}
to close the system.
Furthermore, we need to reconstruct the peculiar velocity
\textstyle u
and energy density

\tilde{\rho}
from the dynamical variables
\tilde{T}^{0\mu}_{\rm pf}
to express the imperfect (viscous)
and Hubble four-forces.
This procedure is described in Sec.
3.4
.
In first place, for a constant
c_{\rm s}^{2}
, the Lorentz factor can be computed in the following way,

\gamma^{2}=\frac{1}{2(1-r^{2})}\left[1-2r^{2}\frac{c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}+\sqrt{1-4r^{2}\frac{c_{\rm s}^{2}}{(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})^{2}}}\ \right]\,,\quad{\rm where\ \ }r^{2}=\frac{\tilde{T}^{0i}_{\rm pf}\tilde{T}^{0i}_{\rm pf}}{({\tilde{T}^{00}_{\rm pf}})^{2}}\,.

(280)

Then, once
\gamma^{2}
is computed, the stress tensor can be reconstructed using

\tilde{T}^{ij}_{\rm pf}=[(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\gamma^{2}u^{i}u^{j}+c_{\rm s}^{2}\delta^{ij}]\,\tilde{\rho}=\frac{\tilde{T}^{0i}_{\rm pf}\,\tilde{T}^{0j}_{\rm pf}}{(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\tilde{\rho}\gamma^{2}}+c_{\rm s}^{2}\,\tilde{\rho}\,\delta^{ij}\,,

(281)

where the energy density
\tilde{\rho}
and the peculiar velocity
\textstyle u
can be
reconstructed from
\tilde{T}^{0\mu}_{\rm pf}
as

\tilde{\rho}=\frac{\tilde{T}_{\rm pf}^{00}}{(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\gamma^{2}-c_{\rm s}^{2}}\,,\qquad u^{i}=\frac{\tilde{T}^{0i}_{\rm pf}}{(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\tilde{\rho}\gamma^{2}}\,.

(282)

An alternative procedure is described in Sec.
6.3
where,
instead of evolving dynamically the stress-energy tensor
components of the perfect fluid,
\tilde{T}^{0\mu}_{\rm pf}
, one
can evolve the combined components of the perfect fluid
and the electromagnetic fields,

\tilde{T}^{0\mu}=\tilde{T}^{0\mu}_{\rm pf}+\tilde{T}^{0\mu}_{\rm EM}
.

Subrelativistic limit

The subrelativistic limit of the conservation form of the MHD equations has been
considered in Sec.
3.4
, where it is shown that terms of order

r^{2}\sim{\cal O}(u^{2})
need to be kept in the time derivatives to lead to the correct limit
in the subrelativistic regime
u^{2}\ll 1
.
The same procedure as the one described in the relativistic conservation form
is taken. In this case, the stress tensor
\tilde{T}^{ij}_{\rm pf}
is related
to the dynamical variables in the following way,

\lim_{u^{2}\ll 1}\tilde{T}^{ij}_{\rm pf}=\frac{1}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\frac{\tilde{T}^{0i}_{\rm pf}\,\tilde{T}^{0j}_{\rm pf}}{\tilde{T}^{00}_{\rm pf}}\biggl(1+\frac{r^{2}c_{\rm s}^{2}}{(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})^{2}}\biggr)+c_{\rm s}^{2}\,\tilde{T}^{00}_{\rm pf}\biggl(1-\frac{r^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\biggr)\,\delta^{ij}\,.

(283)

In this regime, Ohm’s law becomes

\lim_{u^{2}\ll 1}\tilde{J}^{0}=\tilde{\rho}_{e}+\tilde{\sigma}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\,,\qquad\lim_{u^{2}\ll 1}\tilde{J}^{i}=\tilde{\rho}_{e}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+\tilde{\sigma}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\,.

(284)

7.2
Relativistic MHD equations in the non-conservation form

The fully relativistic fluid equations of motion
in the non-conservation form are presented, up to our knowledge,
for the first time, in Sec.
6.4

[cf. Eqs. (
241
) and discussion in Sec.
1
around Eqs. (
2
)],

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\ln\tilde{\rho}\,=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-\frac{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2}}\,(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\tilde{\rho}+\Bigl[(\beta-4)+\frac{1+u^{2}}{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2}}(1-3c_{\rm s}^{2})\Bigr]\,{\cal H}

\displaystyle\,+\frac{1}{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2}}\frac{1}{\tilde{\rho}}\bigl[(\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{0}+\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0})(1+u^{2})-2\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor})\bigr]\,,

(285a)

\displaystyle D_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}}{(1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2})\gamma^{2}}\biggl[c_{\rm s}^{2}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+c_{\rm s}^{2}\frac{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\tilde{\rho}-\frac{1}{\tilde{\rho}}\biggl(\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{0}+\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0}-\frac{2c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot[\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}]\biggr)\biggr]

\displaystyle\,-\frac{c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\ln\tilde{\rho}}{\gamma^{2}}+\frac{1}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\,\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}}{\tilde{\rho}\gamma^{2}}+\frac{3c_{\rm s}^{2}-1}{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2}}\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\,{\cal H}}{\gamma^{2}}\,,

(285b)

where
D_{\tau}=\partial_{\tau}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}
, and
\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{\mu}
and
\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{\mu}

are the imperfect (viscous) and Lorentz four-forces.

Subrelativistic limit

In the subrelativistic limit, Eqs. (
285
) reduce to [cf. Eqs. (
245
)]

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\lim_{u^{2}\ll 1}\partial_{\tau}\ln\tilde{\rho}=

\displaystyle-(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-(1-c_{\rm s}^{2})(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle\,+\frac{1}{\tilde{\rho}}\bigl[\tilde{f}^{0}_{\rm ipf}+\tilde{f}^{0}_{\rm Lor}-2\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor})\bigr]+\bigl[\beta-3(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\bigr]\,,

(286a)

\displaystyle\lim_{u^{2}\ll 1}D_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}=

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\,c_{\rm s}^{2}\biggl[\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+\frac{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\tilde{\rho}\biggr]-\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}}{\tilde{\rho}}\biggl[\tilde{f}^{0}_{\rm ipf}+\tilde{f}^{0}_{\rm Lor}+\frac{2c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor})\biggr]
