\displaystyle\,-\frac{c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\ln\tilde{\rho}+\frac{1}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}}{\tilde{\rho}}+(3c_{\rm s}^{2}-1)\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\,{\cal H}\,.

(286b)

These equations present corrections due to the subrelativistic limit
of
D_{\tau}\ln\gamma^{2}
that, up to our knowledge, have not been
taken into account in previous work (see discussion in Sec.
1

and the explicit corrections in Sec.
6
, cf. Eqs. (
212
) for
c_{\rm s}^{2}={\textstyle{1\over 3}}
,
and in Eqs. (
248
) for a generic constant
c_{\rm s}^{2}
).
The subrelativistic heat flux,
\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{0}
, and viscous forces,
\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{i}
,
are presented in Eq. (
276
). The Lorentz four-force components
are given in Eqs. (
278
) and (
284
),
and
the work against viscous forces can be expressed as [cf. Eq. (
150
)],

\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}}{\tilde{p}+\tilde{\rho}}\simeq-2\,\tilde{\nu}\,\tilde{S}^{ij}\,\tilde{S}_{ij}+\bigl({\textstyle{2\over 3}}\tilde{\nu}-\tilde{\xi}\bigr)\,\tilde{\theta}^{2}\,,

(287)

for homogeneous
\tilde{\nu}
and
\tilde{\xi}
.

In the MHD limit, i.e., when the conductivity is large and the
displacement current can be neglected, the current density that
appears in the Lorentz force is
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}
and
the electric field is computed from Ohm’s law [cf. Eq. (
190
)].
In this limit, when the Alfvén speed can become locally large, one
can include the Boris correction (see Sec.
6.6
) and modify Eq. (
286b
)
to the following

\partial_{\tau}u^{i}={\rm RHS}^{i}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}})-v_{\rm A}^{2}\gamma_{A}(\delta^{ij}-\hat{b}^{i}\hat{b}^{j})\,{\rm RHS}_{j}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}})\,,

(288)

where RHS
(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{})
is the right-hand-side of Eq. (
286b
).
This correction allows to always maintain subluminal Alfvén speeds,
where

v_{\rm A}^{2}=\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}^{2}}{(1+c_{\rm s}^{2})\tilde{\rho}}\,,\qquad\gamma_{A}=\frac{1}{1+v_{\rm A}^{2}}\,.

(289)

7.3
Maxwell equations

Maxwell equations describe the dynamics of the electric and
magnetic fields (see Sec.
5.4
),

\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\,,\qquad\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\,,\qquad\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}=\tilde{J}^{0}\,,\qquad\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=0\,,

(290)

where the four-current
\tilde{J}^{\mu}
is given by the generalized
Ohm’s law.
In terms of the gauge field
A_{\mu}=(\chi,A_{i})
, Maxwell equations
are

\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}=-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}^{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}{}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}{})-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\,,\qquad\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\chi-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}\,,\qquad\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}=\tilde{J}^{0}\,,

(291)

where
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{A}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{A}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{A}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{A}$}}{}
and a particular gauge can be chosen
(e.g.,
\chi=0
in the temporal gauge).
In general, this system of equations (in terms of
\textstyle\tilde{E}
and
\textstyle\tilde{B}

or in terms of
\textstyle\tilde{E}
and
\textstyle A
) can be evolved together with the
equations of motion of the fluid, representing a closed system
of equations.

In the MHD limit of large conductivity, the displacement current can be neglected,
\partial_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}=0
.
Hence, Faraday’s law becomes a constraint equation

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}
,
and Maxwell equations reduce to one
dynamical variable,
\textstyle\tilde{B}
,
described by the magnetic induction equation
(see Sec.
5.5
)

D_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{})+\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\gamma}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}^{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}+\tilde{\eta}\tilde{\rho}_{e}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-\tilde{\eta}\,(1-u^{2})(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\gamma)\,,

(292)

where
\textstyle\tilde{E}
has been substituted by Ohm’s law [cf. Eq. (
190
)],

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{E}$}}{}=\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\gamma}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}-\tilde{\eta}\,\tilde{\rho}_{e}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\,,

(293)

and
we have assumed homogeneous magnetic diffusivity
\tilde{\eta}=1/\tilde{\sigma}
and charge density
\tilde{\rho}_{e}
.
The last term in Eq. (
292
) can explicitly be expressed as

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\gamma=(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\gamma\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}-(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\gamma\,.

(294)

In terms of the vector potential, the induction equation is

D_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\chi+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}{})+\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\gamma}\,\bigl[\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}^{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}{})\bigr]+\tilde{\eta}\,\tilde{\rho}_{e}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\,.

(295)

In the subrelativistic limit (
u^{2}\ll 1
),
the induction equations for
\textstyle\tilde{B}
and
\textstyle A
simplify to

\displaystyle\lim_{u^{2}\ll 1}D_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=

\displaystyle(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{})+\tilde{\eta}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}^{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}+\tilde{\eta}\tilde{\rho}_{e}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\,,

(296a)

\displaystyle\lim_{u^{2}\ll 1}D_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}{}=

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\chi+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}{})+\tilde{\eta}\bigl[\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}^{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}{})\bigr]+\tilde{\eta}\tilde{\rho}_{e}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\,.

(296b)

Finally, the Lorentz force that appears in the fluid equations of
motion [c.f. Eq. (
278
)] is obtained using
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}
,

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}{}
, and taking
\textstyle\tilde{E}
from Ohm’s law [cf. Eqs. (
229
) and (
232
)],

\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0}=\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\gamma}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}^{2}-\tilde{\eta}\,\tilde{\rho}_{e}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{})\,,\qquad\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}=\biggl(\frac{\tilde{\rho}_{e}}{\gamma}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\biggr)\biggl(\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\gamma}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}-\tilde{\eta}\tilde{\rho}_{e}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\biggr)+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{J}$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{B}$}}{}\,.

(297)

Appendix A
Vorticity production

In this appendix, we focus on the vorticity production in the case
of a relativistic fluid with
c_{\rm s}^{2}\sim{\cal O}(1)
.
Let us consider the relativistic momentum equation Eq. (
241b
) for an equation of state
\tilde{p}=c_{\rm s}^{2}\tilde{\rho}
, where the squared speed of sound
c_{\rm s}^{2}

is a constant (e.g.,
c_{\rm s}^{2}={\textstyle{1\over 3}}
for radiation-dominated fluids),

D_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}=\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\Psi}{(1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2})\gamma^{2}}-\frac{1}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\tilde{p}}{\tilde{\rho}\gamma^{2}}+\frac{1}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm tot}}{\tilde{\rho}\gamma^{2}}\,,

(298)

where
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm tot}
refers to any external
forces, e.g., viscous and Lorentz forces

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm tot}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm ipf}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm Lor}
.
We omit the Hubble friction in the momentum equation,
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{H}
, since it vanishes for the choice
\beta=4
.
For compactness, we have defined the scalar
\Psi
as

\Psi=c_{\rm s}^{2}\,\biggl[\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}+\frac{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\tilde{\rho}\biggr]-\frac{1}{\tilde{\rho}}\biggl[\tilde{f}^{0}_{\rm tot}+\frac{2\,c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm tot}\biggr]+(3c_{\rm s}^{2}-1){\cal H}\,,

(299)

where
\tilde{f}_{\rm tot}^{0}=\tilde{f}_{\rm ipf}^{0}+\tilde{f}_{\rm Lor}^{0}
are
the viscous and Lorentz dissipative forces.
We can then take the curl of Eq. (
298
) to find a conservation equation for the
vorticity
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}
,

\displaystyle D_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}}{}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}}{}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}}{}\Psi}{(1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2})\gamma^{2}}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\biggl[\frac{\Psi}{(1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2})\gamma^{2}}\biggr]

\displaystyle\,+\frac{1}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\frac{(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\tilde{p}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm tot})\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}(\tilde{\rho}\gamma^{2})}{\tilde{\rho}^{2}\gamma^{4}}+\frac{1}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm tot}}{\tilde{\rho}\gamma^{2}}\,.

(300)

To find the curl of the convective derivative, we
first take into account that

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}=\bigl[(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}-{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}^{2}\bigr]\,,

(301)

such that
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times[(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}]=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{})
. Then we apply the following identity,

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{})=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}}{}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{})+(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}}{}-(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\,,

(302)

where we have used
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}}{}=0
.
In Eq. (
300
),
we have also used the following identity

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\psi)=\psi\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}}{}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\psi\,,

(303)

which applies
for any scalar
\psi
.

Let us now focus on each of the
last three terms of Eq. (
300
), which
can lead to the production of vorticity even if
\textstyle\omega
is
zero initially,

\lim_{|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}}{}|\to 0}D_{\tau}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\omega$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}}{}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\biggl[\frac{\Psi}{(1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2})\gamma^{2}}\biggr]+\frac{1}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\frac{(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\tilde{p}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm tot})\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}(\tilde{\rho}\gamma^{2})}{\tilde{\rho}^{2}\gamma^{4}}+\frac{1}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm tot}}{\tilde{\rho}\gamma^{2}}\,.

(304)

The first of these terms is proportional to
\Psi
, which
vanishes when
c_{\rm s}^{2}\to 0
(i.e., matter-dominated fluid),
in the absence of Hubble friction, and in the subrelativistic limit,
such that
\tilde{f}_{\rm tot}^{0}
and
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm tot}
can be neglected.
However, when one of these terms is non-zero, vorticity can be
produced from an initial purely compressional (irrotational)
velocity field.
For example, the term arising from
c_{\rm s}^{2}={\textstyle{1\over 3}}
had been pointed
out and studied in
Dahl et al. (2022)
;
Dahl et al. (2024)
, while all the
subrelativistic terms are considered in
Caprini et al. ()
.
The remaining two terms in Eq. (
304
)
correspond to the generalization of the
baroclinic term
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\tilde{p}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\tilde{\rho}

and the vorticity production due to external forces (magnetic fields
or viscosity).
In general, unlike for inviscid
matter-dominated subrelativistic fluids in
flat space-time, where vorticity is a topological invariant described
by Euler equations, vorticity can be produced when one includes
a relativistic equation of state or the expansion of the
Universe, even for a perfect fluid
with no dissipation and neglecting external forces.
In the following, we briefly describe each of the potential
terms that lead to the production of vorticity.

A.1 Baroclinic contribution

The baroclinic term contributing to the production of vorticity is
the following,

\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\tilde{p}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}(\tilde{\rho}\gamma^{2})}{\tilde{\rho}^{2}\gamma^{4}}=\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\tilde{p}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\tilde{\rho}}{\tilde{\rho}^{2}\gamma^{2}}+\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\tilde{p}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}u^{2}}{\tilde{\rho}}\,,

(305)

where we have used
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\gamma^{2}=\gamma^{4}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}u^{2}
.
The first term is the usual subrelativistic baroclinic term
and it becomes zero whenever the pressure is only a function of
\tilde{\rho}
,
while
the second contribution is a relativistic term that can lead to the production
of vorticity even when the first term is zero, as it is proportional to the
misalignment between the pressure and the speed gradients.

A.2 Vorticity production when
c_{\rm s}^{2}\neq 0
and
\tilde{f}^{\mu}_{\rm tot}=\tilde{f}_{H}^{\mu}=0

We now consider the term that can lead to the production of vorticity
when
c_{\rm s}^{2}\neq 0
, in the absence of external forces
\tilde{f}^{\mu}_{\rm tot}=0
and Hubble friction,

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\biggl[\frac{\Psi}{(1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2})\gamma^{2}}\biggr]=\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\Psi}{(1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2})\gamma^{2}}-\frac{c_{\rm s}^{2}-\gamma^{2}}{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}u^{2}}\,\Psi\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}u^{2}\,.

(306)

The second term is a relativistic term that leads to the production of vorticity
with a strength proportional to the misalignment of
\textstyle u
and the gradient
of
u^{2}
.
The first contribution is proportional to

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\Psi=

\displaystyle\,c_{\rm s}^{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times[\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{})]+c_{\rm s}^{2}\frac{1-c_{\rm s}^{2}}{1+c_{\rm s}^{2}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\times[(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle u$}}{}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{})\ln\tilde{\rho}]\,,

(307)

which is, in general, different than zero, so it can lead to the production
of vorticity in the subrelativistic limit.

A.3 Vorticity production when
c_{\rm s}^{2}=0
and
\tilde{f}_{\rm tot}^{\mu}\neq 0

When
c_{\rm s}^{2}=0
, the term proportional to
\Psi
is zero.
The remaining term that can produce vorticity can be split
in the following way,

\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm tot}}{\tilde{\rho}\gamma^{2}}-\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm tot}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}(\tilde{\rho}\gamma^{2})}{\tilde{\rho}^{2}\gamma^{4}}=\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm tot}}{\tilde{\rho}\gamma^{2}}-\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm tot}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}\tilde{\rho}}{\tilde{\rho}^{2}\gamma^{2}}-\frac{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{f}$}}{}_{\rm tot}\times\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\nabla$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}}{}u^{2}}{\tilde{\rho}}\,.

(308)

The first two terms describe the usual subrelativistic
production of vorticity in the presence
of external forces, while the third term corresponds to a relativistic
term producing vorticity that will be non-zero when the external
forces and gradients of
u^{2}
are misaligned.
